2069. THOMAS SEATON was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of September, 1 pair of trousers, value 5s., the goods of John Boards.
JOHN BOARDS. I am a pawnbroker. The prisoner was in the habit of pawning things almost every morning; I missed a great many things, and was at a loss to know where they went - one morning I missed a coat; I found these trousers at another pawnbroker's - I swear they had been stolen from my shop by somebody; they have my mark on them.
WILLIAM ESTILL. I am a pawnbroker. I have a pair of trousers, pawned by the prisoner.
GUILTY. Aged 77. - Confined Two Years.
[trialAccount t18301028-226]
2070. GEORGE SCUTT was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of September, 1 coat, value 10s., the goods of William Thirkell.
MARY ROBINSON. I live opposite the house of William Thirkell. On the morning of the 27th of September I was looking out at window; I saw the prisoner get in at the window, and get out something - I gave an alarm; my son pursued him, and my husband went and got the Policeman.
Prisoner. Q. How far is your window? A. Nearly opposite; I did not state it was one hundred yards from it- the window was partly open; I did not say I did not see your face; I am positive of your person.
THOMAS ROBINSON. I am the son of the witness - she told me of this; I got up, and looked at the window - I saw the prisoner turn round, and pursued him a hundred yards or more - he was carrying this coat in a black bag; he gave it me out when I got up to him, but kept the bag himself.
Prisoner. Q. Did you come up to me? A. Yes; you were stopped near the corner of Redmans-row; it was you that gave me the coat, and you asked me to let you go and said you had nothing; I let you go, and you were taken again.
JOSEPH ALDRIDGE. I am an officer. I was in Stepney-fields; the prisoner was delivering up the coat when I first came in sight of him - he then ran when he saw me; I called Stop thief! a man laid hold of him, but he was very active and got away - I called after him again, and my brother officer turned a corner and took him.
WILLIAM THIRKELL. This is my coat, it laid in a chair in my front room - it was not in any bag.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18301028-227]
2071. JOHN SHIRLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of September, 1 necklace, value 2s., the goods of Richard Robertson, from the person of Sarah Robertson .
JOSIAH ROOME. I am nine years of age. On the 28th of September I was at Mr. Hughes', the grocer, at the corner of Seabright-street; I saw Sarah Robertson and another little girl with her - Sarah Robertson had a necklace on - she cried out, "Boy! beads!" and the prisoner took the necklace off her neck, threw it to another boy behind, and they ran different ways - I had seen the prisoner before - he wanted to take my marbles; I am sure he is the boy - I ran after him with the baby, and gave the alarm; I then went, and took home the baby.
FRANCES ROBERTSON. I am the wife of Richard Robertson; my little girl is two years old - she was at the door while I was in the shop; I heard the alarm, ran, and caught the prisoner - he had nothing then; he had ran perhaps, fifty yards - the necklace was worth perhaps 2s.
Prisoner. I sat down on a stone, and that woman came and took me.
GUILTY. Aged 11. - Whipped, and delivered to a person who engaged to employ him.
[trialAccount t18301028-228]
2072. EDWARD SANNAGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of October, 1 necklace, value 5s., the goods of William Pinder, from the person of Caroline Pinder .
JAMES DUNN. I was in Brownlow-street on the 18th of October; there was a puppet-show - I saw a little girl, and heard a report that some beads were gone from her neck; I dived into the business, and took the prisoner and another into custody; while I had hold of the other, the prisoner stood by, and I saw the beads drop from his person - they had both been close behind the little girl; I found nothing on the other, and let him go - I was close to the prisoner, and am quite positive I saw the beads drop from him.
WILLIAM PINDER. I am the father of Caroline Pinder. She came to me for a halfpenny to see the puppet-show - this necklace was then about her neck, tied with blue cotton and a common gilt snap - I had seen the prisoner before.
Prisoner's Defence. I never was guilty of any thing before, and I do not know what I am brought here for.
GUILTY. Aged 15. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18301028-229]
2073. THOMAS ROBERTS was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of October, 1 hat, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Richard Osborn.
RICHARD OSBORN. I was at the Macclesfield Arms public-house on the 22nd of October; I fell asleep, and when I awoke my hat was gone - the prisoner had been sitting a little distance from me; he returned, and I accused him of it - he denied it; Mr. Spring's young man said he saw a man with a hat on, which he thought was not his- and the next day the prisoner went and showed me where it was - this is the hat; I found it where the prisoner pointed it out.
Prisoner's Defence. He said if I would give him his hat he would liberate me, and say nothing about it.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18301028-230]
2074. CHARLES WILLIS was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of October, 1 coat, value 12s., the goods of Charles Iliff.
CHARLES ILIFF. I am ostler to a gentleman in St. John's-wood-road. I missed a coat out of his coach-house on the 24th of October, at seven o'clock in the evening - the door had been shut, but not latched; the prisoner had been living with my master for two years, but was discharged a week before - this is my coat.
THOMAS SMITH. I am a pawnbroker. This coat was pawned with me by a woman, in the name of Ann Thomas - she is not here.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18301028-231]
2075. JOHN WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of October, 2 coats, value 25s., and 1 waistcoat, value 20s., the goods of John Griffith Morgan and Evan Morgan.
DIANA MORGAN. I am sister of Griffith and Evan Mor gan, who are in partnership as tailor s, and live in Cleveland-street. About seven o'clock in the evening of the 16th of October, I saw the prisoner in the shop, as I was coming out of an adjoining room - he had a bundle in his possession, and asked me for a person named Hanson, a tailor; before I had time to answer he went out with the bundle - I followed him, caught his coat, and gave an alarm; he dropped these clothes as my sister-in-law came to my assistance; he ran up the street, but was taken and brought back - here are two coats and a waistcoat.
MARY MORGAN. I am the wife of Griffith Morgan. My sister followed the prisoner, and I went to her assistance; I saw him drop the bundle, and took it up - it contained these articles, which are the prosecutor's property; we have no person in our employ named Hanson.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18301028-232]
2076. ALICE BYGRAVE and MARY PURSER were indicted for stealing, on the 27th of October, 26 yards of ribbon, value 2s., the goods of Robert Bolton Shaw.
SUSANNAH ELIZABETH SHAW. I am the wife of Robert Bolton Shaw - he is a haberdasher, and lives in Kingsland-road. Between four and half-past four o'clock, on the 27th of October, I saw the prisoners in the shop; my little boy served them with one yard of 1d. pink ribbon; they were in company together, and went away together - when they were gone, I missed some ribbon, and the Policeman brought them back with it in about an hour; I am sure neither of them bought it - this is it.
JOSEPH SHAW. I served the prisoners; they came together - they asked for pink ribbon, and both looked at it; they bought one yard; I did not notice who took it or paid for it - my mother missed the twenty-six yards of ribbon; they had not paid me for it.
JOSEPH BURT. I am a Police-officer. I found this ribbon on Bygrave; I saw them together in Sun-street: they went into two haberdashers' shops - this was about half-past four o'clock; I had heard of this robbery, and took them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
One witness gave Bygrave a good character.
BYGRAVE - GUILTY. Aged 13.
PURSER - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18301028-233]
2077. MARY DUFFEY was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of October, 2 frocks, value 1s.; 3 caps, value 9d.; 1 petticoat, value 6d.: 1 child's cloak, value 1s., and 3 handkerchiefs, value 1s., the goods of George Bonsey, her master.
SARAH BONSEY. I am the wife of George Bonsey - we live in Gloucester-court, Spicer-street. The prisoner was employed to carry loads for us in the market - I had been out on the 27th of October; a person came and said there was a piece of work in my house - I went home, and my mother was taking some of my property from the prisoner, all but two frocks, which I took from her.
SARAH CRAFT. I was at my house, and one of my daughters came and said she saw the prisoner filling her pockets with the prosecutrix's things - I went, saw the prisoner, and took most of these things from her right-hand pocket; the prosecutrix then came, and found two more frocks.
BENJAMIN HANDLEY. I took the prisoner, and found two frocks under her clothes.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. If I had a mind to steal I could have taken more things than that; I thought I would get a few halfpence, but it was my honest intention to bring them back.
GUILTY. Aged 56. - Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18301028-234]
2078. JOHN WILLIAMS and JOHN WOOD were indicted for stealing, on the 27th of April, 3 sovereigns, and 2 gold rings, value 17s., the property of John Michael White.
ISABELLA WHITE. I am the wife of John Michael White - we live in the City-gardens. On the 27th of April Williams came and brought this letter - he said he came from Mr. Horne, to borrow three sovereigns; I asked him if he would wait, as my husband was out - he was there three quarters of an hour, and I asked him a great many questions; at last, in consequence of the letter, and believing he came from Mr. Horne, I gave him the three sovereigns; I know nothing of Wood - this is the letter - he asked if there was any work to go back, and I gave him the two rings.
RICHARD GEORGE STATHAM. I am a constable. I took the prisoners from the information of Mrs. Horne.(Letter read.)
MR. WHITE, You will excuse my sending you this note, but if you could oblige me with the loan of 3l. until Saturday next, as I have a bill come which I must settle this evening; if you can send it by bearer, I will call on Saturday and settle the same. THOMAS R. HORNE.
THOMAS ROBERT HORNE. I never sent this letter or the boy, to get this money - Wood called to ask me where Mrs. White lived.
WILLIAMS - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
WOOD - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18301028-235]
2079. ELIZABETH ROBINSON was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of October, 3 shillings, the monies of John Marshall, from his person.
JOHN MARSHALL. I am a sailor. I was in Ratcliff-highway on the evening of the 28th of October - I was sober; the prisoner and another girl came and asked me to go home with them - I said No; they took hold of me. and the prisoner put her hand into my breeches pocket and went away; I put my hand down and missed my money, which was 3s. - I called out, and the prisoner was taken; I had not lost sight of her.
Prisoner. Q. Can you say I put my hand into your pocket? A. Yes.
SAMUEL DYER. I am a Police-officer. I was on duty - the prosecutor called out, and the prisoner ran; I caught her - the prosecutor appeared sober, and charged her with robbing him of 3s.; two other girls came up, and I heard the money rattling from the prisoner to another - I caught her hand, and found there 4s. 4 1/2d.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw a crowd, and this man was calling the Police - there were three or four girls; I heard money chinking - the Policeman caught me, andsaid he thought I was the girl; he took the money out of another girl's hand - the prosecutor said he did not think I was the girl.
JOHN MARSHALL. I did not say so - I swear she is the girl: it was between eleven and twelve o'clock.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18301028-236]
2080. MARY ANN ROMNEY was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of October, 2 sheets, value 6s.; 1 blanket, value 2s.; 1 clock, value 10s.; 1 hat, value 2s., and 1 teaboard, value 1s., the goods of Joseph Trayling.
The prosecutor did not appear NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18301028-237]
2081. PHOEBE SULLIVAN was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of October, 2 sovereigns, the monies of John Barber, from his person.
JOHN BARBER. I am a mariner. Last Wednesday night I met the prisoner - she accosted me, and I went home and slept with her; I had two sovereigns and some silver when I went with her, and I expected I had the same when I went out with her in the morning - the sovereigns had been wrapped in a bit of paper, and were in my trousers pocket, which I put on the chair by the bed side; I had slept in the night, and we both got up together in the morning - we went out, had a glass of liquor, and then parted; I soon felt a heavy drowsiness come over me - I then went back to her room, and when I got back I missed the two sovereigns out of the paper, and two shillings were put in instead of them; I do not know that I had missed any silver - I had given her some.
WILLIAM DIXON (a blind youth). I know Phoebe Sullivan by her voice; she was nursing my mother - and a little after eight o'clock that evening she gave me two sovereigns to take care of for her.
WILLIAM MARLOW. I am the officer. I took the prisoner on the Thursday morning, and found nothing on her; I took her to the station-house - I made her no promise or threat; but in going to the office she said she had a sovereign and a half at home, and half a sovereign she had spent abroad; she said it was the sailor's money - I went back, found this blind lad, and got the two sovereigns - when I went back she said she merely wanted to frighten him out of half a sovereign; she had not changed it.
Prisoner. My intention was to give them back to him; I never was here in my life.
GUILTY. Aged 29. - Transported for Seven Years
